"你可真是头一号的犟女人——"
"少贫嘴——等我学会了打字，你那办公室——等你有了办公室——可以雇我呀。"
他惊得站住脚，直勾勾瞧着她。她回望他的那副神情，戏谑、笃定与柔情交织，猛地给了他一股排山倒海般的幸福，几乎把他掀了个跟头。

===Original===
“Gee!” he said, his face shining. “Gee﻿—Lindy﻿—”
He had an impulse to catch her up and kiss her right there, on the street corner oblivious to broad daylight and possible observation. Had he done so, spontaneously, on the crest of that emotional wave, the result would doubtless have been different. But the old habit of hardness, which for the instant he had almost escaped, promptly clamped itself down on his exuberance and distorted his natural impulse into a presumably safer substitute. Every act must be sentimentally airtight. The device he adopted to make this one so, lost for them both that surging moment to which the girl would have responded.
“Ain’t it somewhere in the Bible sump’m ’bout turnin’ th’ other cheek?”
Puzzled, her own spell broken, she answered, “You mean﻿—if a man smite you on one cheek, turn him the other also?”
Before she sensed his intention, he had pinioned her arms and kissed her on one cheek. “Well, turn me th’ other one, then,” he grinned.
But Linda could play as safe as he. For answer she snatched herself away, and the sounding smack that met his face must have made the girl’s palm burn.

===Chinese===
"哎哟！"他说，脸都亮了。"哎哟——琳迪——"
他一时冲动，想当场把她搂起来亲一亲，就在街角上，全然不管大天白日、路人瞧不瞧见。倘若他真这么做了，趁着那阵情感的浪头顺水推舟，结局多半会大不一样。可是那副硬汉的老习惯——他方才险些逃开的老习惯——旋即一把摁住了他的兴头，把他天然的冲动扭成了个自以为更稳妥的替代品。每一个举动都必须在感情上滴水不漏。他为求这一下滴水不漏而耍的花招，把姑娘本会应和的那阵翻涌的时刻，给他们两个人都断送了个干净。
"圣经里头是不是有句什么话，讲的是叫人转过另一边脸来？"
她给弄糊涂了，自己的痴劲儿也散了，答道："你是说——有人打你这边的脸，连那边的脸也由他打？"
她还没咂摸出他的用意，他已钳住她两条胳膊，在她一边脸上亲了一口。"那好，把另一边转过来呗。"他咧嘴笑道。
不过琳达求稳的本事不输他。作为回答，她猛地挣脱出去，那一记脆响的耳光抽在他脸上，怕是打得姑娘自己手心发烫。

===Original===
Shocked, strangely hurt within, gigantically helpless without, Shine stood rubbing his cheek and watching her stride indignantly away.
What he eventually said was:
“Now ain’t she a hell of a Christian?”

===Chinese===
夏因愣在当场，心里莫名发疼，空有一身力气使不上，只站在那儿揉着脸蛋，眼看她气冲冲地大步走远。
末了，他憋出这么一句：
"瞧瞧，这也叫顶呱呱的基督徒？"

## Walls. / 墙

### XVIII / 十八

===Original===
On the night when Shine told Jinx and Bubber the story of the battle of Jericho, he had no sooner left Pat’s than another argument was on. Hitherto, Jinx and Bubber’s nocturnal enmity had always ended at least without catastrophe; tonight catastrophe descended upon them, and the thing which each sought to divert by the very extravagance of his quarrel was by the same extravagance rendered inevitable. Tonight they came to blows.
Jinx started it.
“There now, you dumb Oscar,” he said to Bubber with great relish, in a voice that carried throughout Pat’s barroom.
“There now whut, jackass?”
“Didn’ I tell y’?”
“You ain’t tole me nuthin’﻿—and if you did, it ’twasn’t nuthin’ nohow.”

===Chinese===
夏因给金克斯和布伯讲完耶利哥之战的那个晚上，他前脚刚离开帕特莫尔的店，一场新的争执便开了场。在此之前，金克斯和布伯夜夜为敌，好歹总没闹出大祸；这天夜里，大祸降临到了他们头上——两人各自想用吵得惊天动地的法子岔开的那件事，偏偏又让这惊天动地给逼到了眼前，躲不掉了。这天夜里，他们动起手来。
挑头的是金克斯。
"瞧见没，你这个哑巴奥斯卡，"他冲着布伯说，说得津津有味，嗓门大得整个帕特莫尔的酒馆都听得见。
"瞧见没什么，蠢驴？"
"我没跟你说来着？"
"你啥也没跟我说——就算说了，横竖也不算个啥。"

===Original===
“I tole you﻿—” Jinx spaced his words for emphasis, “that nex’ thing we knowed she’d have ’im goin’ in d’ main door of d’ church﻿—and whut ’d you say? ‘Aw no. Ain’ no gal go’n’ do nuthin’ like that to *that* boogy. Hard boogy, he is.’ Thass whut you said. Yea. And look. He comes in and tells us ev’ything d’ damn preacher said. Don’ leave out nuthin’.”
“That don’ prove he went in d’ main door,” argued Bubber with overacted patience. “He could ’a’ come down through d’ skylight f’ all I know.”
“Like a big black angel, I s’pose?” said Jinx and grinned with surrounding laughter.
“Yea﻿—or a long-laigged, speckle-face giraffe,” retorted Bubber, swelling.
Jinx grew sombre. “That’s d’ trouble with a li’l round black hippo like you. All give and no take. When you kid me I kin take it. When I kid you you can’t.”
“You don’ seem to be taken’ that so good,” said Bubber. “Don’ nobody git no madder’n you do.”

===Chinese===
"我跟你说来着——"金克斯一字一顿，好加重分量，"我说的是，转眼她就要让他从教堂正门往里走了——可你怎么说的？『哦不。哪个姑娘也甭想对*那位*老黑使这种手段。硬骨头老黑，他可是。』你就是这么说的。是啊。你再瞧。他进来把那个该死的牧师讲的话一五一十全倒给了咱们，一句没落下。"
"那可证明不了他是从正门进去的，"布伯拿出一份做过了头的耐心来争辩，"保不齐人家是从天窗上下来的呢。"
"我猜啊，像个黑乎乎的大天使？"金克斯说着咧嘴一笑，四下里的哄笑跟着响起。
"是啊——要么就像个长腿杆、麻子脸的长颈鹿，"布伯梗着脖子回敬，胸脯气鼓鼓的。
金克斯脸色沉了下来。"你这又黑又圆的小河马就是这毛病。光许你逗人，不许人逗你。你拿我开涮，我受得住；我拿你开涮，你就受不住。"
"你眼下这副样子，也不像多受得住啊，"布伯说，"天底下没人比你火气更大了。"

===Original===
“No?﻿—look at y’ now. ’Bout to bus’ open and spatter d’ whole bar room with ink.”
“I kin remember,” Bubber returned, “when you didn’ act like nobody’s long lost brother. Never will fo’get that night you got so mad you started slippin’ me in d’ dozens.”
This was approaching dangerous ground, this reference to their own reactions. To quarrel over subjects in general was bad enough; to quarrel over each other might be disastrous. It brought them closer to the truth about themselves, yet not quite close enough; it did not reach the actual sore, it only lifted off the scab.
“Well you oughter been slipped,” Jinx said. “Any bird can’t take kiddin’ no better’n that needs to be kidded and kidded hard.”
The customary comments accompanied this discourse:
“Tell ’em ’bout it!”

===Chinese===
"没有吗？——瞧瞧你自个儿吧。眼看要涨破肚皮，把一肚子的墨汁溅得满酒馆都是。"
"我还记得，"布伯回敬道，"你也未必总像谁家走散多年的亲兄弟。那天夜里你气疯了，冲着我玩起骂娘游戏（the dozens）来，那一晚我一辈子忘不了。"
这就快踩到危险地带了——把话头引到了两人自己的老账上。为些不相干的事吵吵已属糟糕；冲着对方本人吵，只怕要出大乱子。这把他们往自己的真相跟前推近了一步，却还不够近；没碰到真正的痛处，只揭开了结的痂。
"你本来就欠骂，"金克斯说，"哪个人受不住玩笑受成这样，就该狠狠地多逗他几回。"
照例的看客议论随之而来：
"跟他们好好说说！"

===Original===
“That means fight in my home.”
“Grease us twice!”
“They jes’ foolin’. If they meant it they’d both be daid by now.”
“Me, I’m bettin’ on Long Boy. He’ll wrap hisself ’round Squatty and squeeze all th’ ambition out’n ’im.”
Bubber challenged, “Well﻿—you better not slip me ag’in.”
“No?” said Jinx like a small boy who has been dared to knock off the chip. “No? Well﻿—yo’ granddaddy was a mule﻿—Now﻿—what you got to say ’bout that?”

===Chinese===
"这是要在我家里开打了。"
"给咱上两遍油！"
"他俩就是闹着玩。要是来真的，这会儿早都死透了。"
"要我说，我押长条儿。他会把自己缠上矮墩墩，把那一身野心全给挤出来。"
布伯下了战书："行啊——你最好别再招我。"
"不招？"金克斯说，活像个在那套肩头放木片的把戏里受了激、非伸手去打不可的小男孩。"不招？好啊——你爷爷是头骡子——来——这话你怎么接？"

===Original===
Bubber said nothing. Instead he moved toward Jinx with surprising ease and mysterious rapidity and suddenly Jinx doubled forward from the force of an almost invisible blow to the midriff. “What *you* go’n’ say ’bout *that*?” Bubber asked, looking belligerently up into Jinx’s astounded face.
Not quite certain whether this was serious or make-believe, Jinx reached mechanically forward and gathered Bubber’s neck and shoulders in an embrace usually reserved for pianos. Failing to twist himself free, Bubber began swinging away at the other’s kidneys, and in a moment the tussle removed from the atmosphere all suggestion of possible jest.
“Look a yeh!” somebody gasped.
“They ain’t roughin’ sho’ nuff, is they?”
“They ain’ playin’ hopscotch.”
“Well, ain’t this sump’m?”

===Chinese===
布伯没接话。他反倒出奇从容、快得莫名其妙地朝金克斯逼过去，突然一记几乎看不见的拳头打在心口窝上，金克斯顿时折成了对虾。"*你*打算怎么接*这一下*？"布伯仰起脸，好斗地望着金克斯那张惊呆的脸。
金克斯还没弄清这是真打还是假闹，手已经习惯性地伸过去，一把搂住布伯的脖颈和肩膀——这一搂平日是留给钢琴的。布伯拧身挣不脱，便抡开拳头照着对方的腰眼乱捶，不消片刻，这场缠斗就把空气里最后一点玩笑的影子挤了个干净。
"快瞧呐！"有人倒抽一口气。
"他俩该不是真动手了吧？"
"总不是在跳房子。"
"嘿，这可真有瞧头了。"

===Original===
But before either could damage the other, Pat, who was an excellent manager and always at the spot that needed him most, had heard the commotion from the next room and hurried to the scene. Pat was not bad with his hands himself, and it is significant that with apparent ease he managed quickly to separate them.
“What the hell you think this is?” he inquired, as for a moment they stood off from each other glaring.
“Jes’ git out d’ way, thass all,” said one.
“He been cryin’ fo’ it﻿—now he gon’ git it,” vowed the other.
“Not here he ain’t,” Pat decided. “Look,” he pointed, “Y’all see that door? All right. I told y’ once before the nex’ time you wanted to settle sump’n I was go’n’ put you in the cellar and let the best man come up.” He strode to the door, unlocked and opened it, and pressed a button. “Come on, if you mean it﻿—Come on.”
Neither was willing to admit that he did not mean it, and in another moment the gaping bystanders saw them disappear through the cellar door, which Pat promptly closed behind them.

===Chinese===
不过还没等谁伤着谁，帕特就赶到了。他是位顶好的掌柜，哪儿最需要他，他人就在哪儿——隔壁屋里听见动静，人已经抢到了现场。帕特自己的拳脚也不含糊，而且耐人寻味的是，他没费什么力气就把两人拆开了。
"你们当这儿是什么地方？"他质问道。两人暂时分开，隔着距离互相瞪着。
"闪开就得了，没别的，"一个说。
"他早就欠收拾了——这回非收拾不可，"另一个发了狠。
"在我这儿不行，"帕特拍了板。"瞧，"他一指，"都瞧见那扇门了吧？好。我说过的，往后你们再要解决什么过节，我就把你们俩搁进地窖，谁行谁自己上来。"他大步走到门边，开锁，拉开门，摁了下按钮。"来呀，要来真的就上——来呀。"
谁也不肯承认自己不是来真的，转眼间，张着嘴看热闹的众人就眼睁睁瞧着他们消失在地窖门里，帕特随手把门关了个严实。

===Original===
“Well, what do y’ know ’bout that?”
“Ain’t this a dog?”
“Salty dog, I mean.”
“Damn if d’ worm ain’t turned.”
“Yea﻿—but which a one is d’ worm?”
---

===Chinese===
"嘿，这算怎么回事？"
"这算什么事儿，真是条狗。"
"还是条咸狗，我说。"
"他娘的，虫子也有翻身的时候。"
"是啊——可哪一条才是虫子来着？"
---

===Original===
The bystanders crowded about the door, listening. Pat, grinning, kept his hand on the knob, his ear against the panel. The others pressed forward: a lean black boy as tall as Pat, with tight slick skin and wide, white, shifting eyes; a thin, short tan-skinned lad of twenty, with a sharp face half hidden by a voluminous, lopsided cap; a paunchy old brown fellow in shirt sleeves and suspenders, with puffed cheeks and rolling pop-eyes; a long, thin, senilely crouching grandad with the complexion of a mummy and a gloating, toothless grin; a parchment-covered gambler, a tea-colored card-shark, a khaki-skinned pickpocket easing one hand into a pompous racing-man’s pocket, a dozen others, all surging forward, all listening with arched brows or grins of relish. This was gonna be good, this was. Them two guys meant blood.
Most of these, hearing nothing, presently fell back commenting:
“Bet on the long boy!”
“Give you odds.”
“Don’ tell *me*﻿—that jasper can *fight*.”
“Squatty’ll wear him down, though.”

===Chinese===
看热闹的把门围了个水泄不通，侧耳听着。帕特咧着嘴，一只手搭在门钮上，耳朵贴着门板。其余的人一个劲儿往前挤：一个跟帕特一般高的精瘦黑人小伙，皮肤紧绷油亮，一双白眼珠滴溜乱转；一个二十来岁、黄褐皮肤的瘦矮少年，尖脸让一顶歪戴着的大帽子遮去半张；一个衬衫袖子配背带裤、大腹便便的棕皮老家伙，腮帮子鼓鼓的，一双暴眼直打转；一个又长又瘦、老态佝偻的爷字辈，面色如木乃伊，咧着没牙的嘴幸灾乐祸；还有个羊皮纸脸的赌徒、一个茶色的牌油子、一个卡其肤色的扒手正把一只手慢慢送进一位神气活现的马票贩子兜里，另外还有十来号人，全都往前涌，全都竖着眉毛或是咧着嘴听得有滋有味。这下有好戏了，没错。那两个家伙是要见血的。
这些人多半什么也没听见，不多会儿便退下来议论：
"押长条儿！"
"让你几个点。"
"别跟我白话——那家伙能*打*。"
"可矮墩墩能耗死他。"

===Original===
“I knowed they’d ask f’ each other sooner or later﻿—”
“Too bad now.”
“Thass the reason I never kid nobody﻿—might have to *make* him take it, see?”
“Wonder if they’ll cut?”
“Can’t tell what a guy’ll do when he’s losin’.”
“Who’ll move pianers tomorrer?”

===Chinese===
"我早说过，他俩早晚得找上对方——"
"现在可惜了。"
"所以我从不逗人——逗急了还得*逼*人家咽下去，明白不？"
"你说会不会动刀子？"
"人一到输的时候，什么干不出来。"
"明儿个的钢琴谁搬？"

===Original===
“Better git yo’ mop out, Pat.”
“Anybody sent for the ambulance?”
“Ain’ got a chance in the world﻿—”
“Five bucks says he *is*﻿—”
“Who﻿—String-bean?”
“Put yo’ money wha’ yo’ mouth is﻿—”

===Chinese===
"帕特，快把拖把预备下吧。"
"有人去叫救护车了吗？"
"横竖是一点指望没有——"
"五块钱赌他准得——"
"谁——豆角儿？"
"有钱别光说嘴，押上来——"

===Original===
It seemed an endless time, but nobody’s eyes left the door for long. Stories suggested by the present affair began to be told, sudden gusts and flurries of laughter swept the room. Argument ensued over the nature of the quarrel﻿—How had it begun? So﻿—The hell it had﻿—it was like this﻿—Good thing: those two were a constant pain in the what’s-a-name with their continuous quarrel. Over a woman, hey? Huh﻿—jes’ goes to show y’﻿—
Pat was called away from his post by some duty in the pool room. He made sure the cellar door was locked and went about his business, promising to return in time for the rest of the fun.
Another long wait followed﻿—Hear anything? Not a damn thing. Fools must’a gone down there and kilt each other. Remember the night Sam Tyler and Joe West got hooked up? Yea. Waitin’, they was, in the same hotel. The head waiter give Sam a check that should ’a’ been Joe’s, so Joe was sore to start with. Well the man ordered Washington pie, see? You know﻿—that white stuff with whoop’ cream all over it. And Sam brought *chocolate* pie by mistake. So the fay man looked up at Sam, he did, and turned up his nose, like, and says, “Waiter, I ordered George. You’ve brought me Booker.” Well, Joe heard it and when he got through kiddin’ Sam ’bout it, ’twasn’t nothin’ left for ’em to do but fight. Brother, I mean, neither one of ’em ever got over that scrap.﻿—Judas Priest﻿—it’s been three-quarters of an hour! Nary a sound. Better get Pat﻿—thought he was coming back so soon? He was, but he got in a argument with Boody Mullins over a protection-fee. Well, let’s go get him for Chris’ sake﻿—them two damn fools may be tricklin’ all over the floor by now.﻿ ﻿…
---

===Chinese===
时间像是熬不到头，可谁的眼睛也没离开那扇门太久。由眼前这桩事引出来的掌故开始一个接一个地讲，一阵阵哄笑在屋里横扫。跟着又争起这场架的来龙去脉——怎么起的头？是这么着——放屁，明明是那么着——听我说——好在这俩货没完没了地斗嘴，早就是众人嘴里那什么部位的常年病根了。为个娘们儿，嗬？哼——这就可见了——
台球房里有点事，把帕特从岗位上叫走了。他先查看地窖门锁好没有，这才去忙他的，还答应赶在后半场好戏开锣前回来。
又是一阵漫长的干等——听见动静没有？屁都没有。这俩傻子保准是下去把彼此给宰了。记得萨姆·泰勒跟乔·韦斯特干起来的那晚不？记得。他俩是同一家旅馆的侍应生。领班把该给乔的那张支票发给了萨姆，乔打一开始就憋着火。后来那位客人点的是华盛顿派，明白不？就是那种白花花的、浇满了鲜奶油的点心。萨姆错端上来的却是*巧克力*派。那位白人老爷抬眼瞧了瞧萨姆，真是那样，鼻孔朝了天，慢悠悠来了句："服务员，我点的是乔治，你给我端来的却是布克。"嘿，乔听见了，等他把萨姆好一顿取笑，俩人除了打一架，再没第二条路可走。老兄，我是说，那一场打完，俩人这辈子都没缓过劲来。——老天爷——都过去三刻钟了！一点动静没有。快去叫帕特——他不是说马上回来？是来着，可他跟布迪·马林斯为保护费抬上杠了。那就看在老天爷份上自己去请吧——那两个该死的傻蛋，这会儿血怕是要流得满地都是了。 ……
---

===Original===
Patmore came hurriedly in from the pool room, flanked by the two who’d summoned him. He paused a moment to listen, his ear against the door. “I hear sump’n,” he said. “Wonder is﻿—?” and at once unlocked and opened the door.
Everyone had pressed forward behind Pat, but now they all fell back, and as a lane opened through their midst, Jinx was seen framed in the doorway. He was swaying a little from side to side even though he attempted to steady himself against the door frame, and there was a far-off vacancy in his eyes that made him seem completely unaware of those who stood and stared at him. No one said anything, no one moved to help him, as he relinquished his support and started uncertainly forward.
He took four or five grotesque tottering steps, then his legs and feet seemed to get all tangled like those of a fly trying to escape sticky paper, and rather slowly, he sank to the floor and lay crumpled in a twisted, senseless heap.
Pat, who alone of all the onlookers could afford to take an active hand in this matter, started toward that crumpled heap. A sound behind him brought him up short and he turned with the others to see the short broad form of Bubber come into and through the doorway.
Bubber looked decidedly dazed, yet not so much so as had Jinx, and the unsteadiness of his bearing was somewhat modified by his rotundity. His progress through the crowd toward his prone enemy resembled that of a pool ball through a scattered field of its fellows, kissing first this one then that and accordingly zigzagging forward from side to side; like the other balls, his fellows each withdrew a little at each glancing impact, not one extending a supporting hand or revealing a sympathetic impulse. Even Pat did not offer to catch him when he reached Jinx’s figure, tripped over Jinx’s feet, and fell across Jinx’s body.
Then curious things happened.

===Chinese===
帕特莫尔从台球房匆匆赶来，左右两边跟着搬他来的那两位。他停了停，耳朵贴门听了听。"我听见点动静，"他说，"不知是不是——"说着当即开锁，拉开了门。
方才人人都挤在帕特背后，这会儿却齐刷刷退了开去，人堆中间让出一条道来，只见金克斯嵌在门框里。他左右直晃，一手扒着门框想撑住自己，眼神里一片空茫，恍恍惚惚像全然不知有人站在那儿盯着他瞧。谁也没吭声，谁也没上前扶他，他松开手，脚步不稳地朝前挪来。
他东倒西歪、怪模怪样地挪了四五步，两条腿两只脚忽然像粘蝇纸上挣扎的苍蝇一样乱作一团，接着慢慢地瘫倒在地，蜷成扭曲的一堆，人事不省。
众看客里头，也只有帕特一个人还管得起这桩闲事，他朝那团蜷着的身子走过去。身后一声响动让他猛地收住脚，他跟着众人回头，只见布伯那矮阔的身影穿过门洞，走了进来。
布伯瞧着分明也是晕头转向，只是不像金克斯那么厉害，加上一身滚圆，站不稳的样子也少了几分。他从人堆里朝趴着的对头一路蹭过去，那走法活像一颗台球穿行在散了阵的同伙中间，蹭蹭这个，碰碰那个，于是左一歪右一斜地曲折前进；那些"同伙"台球也一样，每回擦碰都各自缩让一下，没有一个伸手扶他一把，也没一个露出半点怜悯的意思。等他蹭到金克斯跟前，绊上金克斯的脚，整个儿栽在金克斯身上时，连帕特也没伸手去接。
接着，怪事来了。

===Original===
Jinx, roused by the jolt of Bubber’s fall, stirred drowsily with a movement that rolled Bubber off to one side, and Bubber was heard to murmur stupidly, “Ain’ nuthin’ to fight about, boogy. Ain’t you my boy?”
Pat called abruptly to a bystander for help, and together they reached down and raised Jinx to his feet. He opened his eyes for a moment, then, as if realizing the futility of trying to see anything, allowed his heavy lids to drop again. They got him on to a chair and his head sagged limply forward.
As they were in the act of turning to render similar assistance to Bubber something halted them half-about and they exchanged puzzled and apprehensive looks. Everyone exchanged similar glances with his neighbor, gazed at Jinx’s sagging form in a fear that grew into conviction; for in that moment the something happened again, as if to substantiate itself by repetition: A shudder took hold on Jinx’s body, shook it from below upwards, halted in his throat with a little choking sound that seemed almost to break his neck.
“Death rattle﻿—Jesus﻿—!” somebody muttered. One or two peripheral observers near the door eased stealthily out. “Ain’ goin’ be no witness in no murder case﻿—no *suh*.”
Scowling, Pat stepped forward, seized Jinx’s shoulder, shook him, called him, pushed up his lids with a thumb. Each lid, released, drooped slowly resolutely shut. Pat frisked Jinx’s clothing, palpated him, searched swiftly but futilely for the wound that must have been dealt; swung around to find Bubber on hands and knees trying to rise, laid hold and yanked him to his feet. Bubber stood teetering like an exercising-ball, stared sleepily about, said, “Where-my-boy?” and unceremoniously sat down unanswered. Pat strode through the cellar door and disappeared down the stairs.
Somebody now searched Bubber for a weapon, and somebody else said Pat had gone to find it. Periodically a spasmodic shudder almost jerked Jinx off his chair. Nobody seemed to know what to do, everyone was helpless.

===Chinese===
金克斯让布伯这一摔震醒过来，迷迷糊糊动弹了一下，把布伯滚到了一边，只听布伯傻乎乎地咕哝："有啥好打的，老哥。咱哥俩不是好兄弟吗？"
帕特猛地喊了个看客来搭手，两人合力把金克斯从地上架了起来。他睁了睁眼，随即像是明白看什么都白搭，又让沉重的眼皮耷拉下去。他们把他挪到一把椅子上，他的脑袋有气无力地垂向前胸。
他们正要转过身去，照样搭布伯一把，有什么动静让两人转了一半便僵住，面面相觑，又迷惑又发怵。人人跟身旁的人交换着眼色，盯着金克斯那瘫垂的身子，心里的害怕渐渐坐实；因为就在那一刻，那动静又来了，像是要靠重演一遍给自己作证：一阵颤抖攫住金克斯的身子，从下往上把他抖了一遍，最后卡在喉咙里，发出一声近乎拧断脖子的咯咯闷响。
"临死的痰音——老天爷——！"有人低声道。门边一两个外围的看客悄悄溜了出去。"老子才不去当什么凶案证人——*不*去。"
帕特皱着眉抢上前来，抓住金克斯的肩膀摇他、喊他，用拇指撑开他的眼皮。每只眼皮一松手，便又慢慢悠悠、斩钉截铁地阖上。帕特在金克斯身上一通摸索、按捏，飞快却徒劳地找那一处必有的伤口；一转身，瞧见布伯正手脚并用地想爬起来，便一把揪住，把他拽了起来。布伯站那儿摇摇晃晃，像只健身用的皮球，睡眼惺忪地四下望了望，说了句"我兄弟呢？"，也没人答话，他就一屁股坐了下去。帕特大步穿过后窖门，消失在楼梯下头。
这会儿有人在布伯身上搜起凶器来，又有人说帕特下楼找的就是这个。金克斯隔一阵便痉挛似的抖上一把，差一点儿从椅子上给抖下去。谁也不知道该干什么，人人都束手无策。

===Original===
“Must a strangled ’im, huh?”
“Seem like it﻿—chokes off his breath.”
“Jes’ goes to show y’﻿—”
Presently Pat returned and came into the circle with ominous deliberateness. He stood for a moment looking down on the helpless pair, nodding his head in mingled conviction and disgust. Then he held up what he had found downstairs, a round quart bottle with perhaps a half-inch of whiskey left in its bottom.
“Give it to Jinx,” urged a bystander. “Might stop that rattle yet﻿—”
“Rattle, hell,” said Pat. “That jigaboo ain’t got a thing but the hiccups.” He set the bottle on the bar counter with a sarcastic thump. “That,” he growled glumly, “is the only damn thing they hit. They found a case.”

===Chinese===
"八成是掐的，嗬？"
"看着像——气儿都给他掐住了。"
"这就可见了——"
不多会儿，帕特回来了，不紧不慢地走进人圈，透着一股不祥的郑重。他站了片刻，低头瞧着那没了辙的一对，又是心下了然又是恶心地直点头。然后他举起下楼寻着的物证：一只圆滚滚的一夸脱酒瓶，瓶底兴许还剩半英寸威士忌。
"给金克斯灌一口，"有看客撺掇，"兴许还能压住那痰音——"
"痰音，见鬼，"帕特说，"那小子屁事没有，就是打嗝。"他把瓶子往吧台上一墩，带着讥讽。"这个，"他阴着脸嘟哝，"才是他们俩唯一打着的东西。他俩摸着一箱酒了。"

### XIX / 十九

===Original===
The fact that Linda had taken the job in Fred Merrit’s house as soon as it was available seemed to Shine, like the slap, a mere gesture of defiance, as a matter of fact rather complimentary and encouraging. But the fact that she stubbornly withheld her company and had done so now for two weeks seemed an unnecessary emphasis of her already defined position.
And because it was for him an entirely new experience, for which his knowledge of women contained no therapy, his own futile resentment rendered him daily more and more violent. He worked harder and played harder and knew that nothing ailed him; but with a stubbornness greater than Linda’s he refused to admit to himself that the girl had anything at all to do with the change. No mamma in this man’s world was tight enough to put it on *him*.
Bess, the great van, became a willing mistress, and from her he derived a sort of unconfessed consolation; took to driving her at top speed whenever conditions permitted: when traffic was light and fast and Bess was empty; literally hurled her, roaring like a fire truck, along Seventh Avenue’s asphalt; and when opportunity presented, took her over to the Speedway for a rattling headlong romp. On such occasions if Jinx and Bubber were present, they would exchange wise looks and apprehensive grimaces, and Bubber invited annihilation one day when, on narrowly missing a coal truck, he asserted that it just wasn’t good arithmetic for no three men to commit suicide over one woman.
---

===Chinese===
琳达等那差事一空出来就进了弗雷德·梅里特家的门——这件事在夏因看来，跟那记耳光一样，不过是逞一时意气，说实在的倒还带着点恭维和鼓劲的意思。可她随后死活不肯再搭理他，一晃就这么晾了他两个礼拜，这就不免把她已经挑明的立场又描了一遍。
而这于他是桩全新的经历，他对女人的那点见识里翻不出半张药方，于是那份徒劳的怨气便一天天把他熏得越来越暴。他干活更狠，玩得也更狠，并且认定自己什么毛病也没有；只是那股拧劲儿比琳达的还大，他死也不肯向自己承认，这变化跟那姑娘有半个铜板的关系。这世上的娘们儿，还没哪个精得能把这盆水泼到*他*头上。
贝斯那辆大货车，倒成了一位随叫随到的相好，夏因从她身上讨得一种不肯明言的安慰：但凡路况允许，他就载着她狂飙——车流稀落、车快人空的时候；载着她像救火车似的咆哮着，一路扫过第七大道的柏油路面；赶上机会，还带她去滨河快车道撒一场叮咣乱响的野欢。逢这种时候，金克斯和布伯若在车上，必要交换几个心照不宣的眼色和提心吊胆的鬼脸。有一天险些蹭上一辆运煤车，布伯发话说，为一个女人赔上三条人命，这笔账横竖算不过来——这话差点让他当场被灭口。
---

===Original===
But the zest with which Shine drove Bess did not give him sufficient relief, left him still unsatisfied, like the deep but ineffectual breathing of a man suffering acute air-hunger. Hence his whole behavior took on a reckless vehemence, and whether he laughed or cursed, worked, drank, or gambled, he did so to excess.
Ordinarily he used two belts around an upright piano to be hoisted; two belts surrounding the treacherous instrument near either lateral end, a cable joining either belt to a central metal ring. When the tackle was hooked into this ring and raised, the two short cables became the legs of an isosceles triangle, the apex of which was the ring and the base the top of the piano. This arrangement was absolutely proof against tilting and slipping.
Now however he decided, jes’ for meanness, to dispense with approximately half of this apparatus and used only a single belt about the middle of the piano. It pleased him then to stand off and dare the blam-blam thing to slip.
Ordinarily when he drank it was with a modicum of caution. No sense getting drunk down. The way to lick liquor was to hit it and run﻿—no man was lined with copper. Drop in on one of these new young doctors that had to write “scrips” to make it; or go to one of these drugstores that had prescriptions already written and could sell you the best rye right off at five or six bucks a pint. On thirty-five bucks you wouldn’t be able to do that but once a week, and so you’d be pretty sure to take it easy.
Now, however, he told himself he could drink anything anybody else could drink, and drink as much of it, too; sought out the venders of synthetic corn and gin and drowned himself in the pale stuff; and cursed to find that he awoke the mornings afterward without even so much as a headache.
Ordinarily, when he played blackjack in Pat’s back room, he played with a definite system: started with the minimum stake, doubled three rounds, then passed. Above all he never hit seventeen.

===Chinese===
可夏因开贝斯的那股痛快并不解渴，过后反倒更空落，像害了急性缺氧的人做的那种深而无用的呼吸。于是他整个行事都带上了一股豁出去的狠劲，笑也笑到头，骂也骂到底，干活、喝酒、耍钱，样样都往过量里去。
平日里吊立式钢琴，他都用两条吊带：两条带子分头缠住那件居心叵测的乐器，各在琴身一端靠里，一条钢缆各连一根带子，汇到当中一只金属环上。吊钩钩进这只环往上一提，两条短缆便成了等腰三角形的两条腰，环是顶角，琴顶是底边。这套布置，翻也好滑也好，一概无隙可乘。
如今他却拿定主意，就为使坏，把这套家什差不多废去一半，只拿一条带子拦腰捆琴。捆完他还要退开几步站着，挑衅那件破玩意儿，看它敢不敢滑。
平日里喝酒，他喝得有分寸。犯不着把自己灌倒。制酒的诀窍是打了就跑——人这副皮囊里可没衬铜皮。顺道找个新挂牌的年轻大夫，人家也得靠开处方刨食；要不就上那几家处方现成写好的药房，上好的黑麦威士忌当场就卖，一品脱五六块钱。挣三十五块钱一礼拜，这么个喝法顶多来一回，你自然就知道悠着点了。
如今他却告诉自己，别人灌得下去的，他都灌得下去，灌多少也随他；专去找卖人工玉米威士忌、人工杜松子酒的贩子，一头扎进那些寡淡的玩意儿里泡着；泡完骂娘——第二天早上一觉醒来，居然连头疼都不带一下的。
平日里在帕特后屋玩二十一点，他玩得有章法：起手押最小注，加倍三轮便收；尤其绝不在十七点上要牌。

===Original===
Now he played with no regard for rules or the laws of chance; doubled often five times straight, “stopped” the bank at every opportunity, and invariably hit a soft seventeen and usually a hard one as well.
None of these devices satisfied. Not a piano slipped, none of the liquor proved to be poison, and at the end of a week, his blackjack stood him eighty-six berries to the good.
In the midst of these exaggerated reflexes, an order came to the office of Isaacs’ Transportation Company for the removal of one load of valuable furniture from Fred Merrit’s country house to his residence on Court Avenue. Old man Isaacs was off duty, ill abed with a bad heart, otherwise Shine would have had the boss appoint a new foreman. Finding this impossible, he told himself that no girl’s presence was going to make him dodge a job any damn how.
There was, nevertheless, an unmistakable reluctance in his piloting of Bess this morning. Merrit’s place was only a dozen miles north of New York, but it took Bess two hours to get there. Once arrived, there was much palaver about the best way to negotiate the terrain.
“This place jes’ sprawls all over this hill,” observed Bubber. “Looks like a flock o’ hencoops. How we go’n’ git up yonder?”
The question was settled by uproarious but careful navigation of a steep side road which led to a plateau behind the flock of hencoops. Here they were greeted by Mrs. Arabella Fuller, who began at once to wheeze interminable directions.

===Chinese===
如今他全不管什么规矩、什么概率；一连加倍五轮是常事，逮着机会就"停庄"，回回都在软十七上要牌，多半还捎上硬十七。
这些招数一概不解气。钢琴一架也没滑，酒没有一口算得上毒药，到了周末一结账，二十一点倒净赢了他八十六块。
就在这满身过剩的反应当中，艾萨克斯运输公司接了一单活：一批贵重家具，从弗雷德·梅里特的郊区别墅搬往法院大道的住宅。老艾萨克斯犯了心脏病，告假卧床，不然夏因本可以让老板另点一位工头。眼见这招不成，他暗自拿定主意：娘们在场，也休想叫他躲活儿。
可这天上午，他掌着贝斯的舵，那份不情愿瞒不了人。梅里特的宅子就在纽约以北十几英里，贝斯却走了两个钟头才到。到了地方，怎么把车挪上那片地，又好一番口舌。
"这宅子满山乱摊，"布伯点评道，"瞧着跟一窝鸡笼似的。咱们怎么上那上头去？"
这一问，靠的是一条陡峭的岔道——一路鸡飞狗跳又小心翼翼——才算了结，岔道通到鸡笼群背后的一片高台。迎接他们的是阿拉贝拉·富勒太太，她当场就喘着大气、没完没了地指点起来。

===Original===
Eventually, in spite of all Mrs. Fuller said, the load was on, each piece swaddled partly in quilting, and partly in that lady’s verbiage, which seemed to hover about it long after Bess was headed back townward:
“Yes﻿—that goes﻿—that’s a picture of his mother﻿—the onliest one he’s got, so be awful careful. I know he’d die if he lost it. Take care o’ that if you lose all the rest. Now be careful﻿—you’ll never care how you handle things and them table laigs’ll snap off if you sneeze at ’em﻿—that’s a genuine redwood table and you know them’s expensive﻿—look out f’ that vase!﻿—the way y’all handle things, anybody’d know they wasn’t yourn. Chile, that vase cost mo’ ’n yo’ foot﻿—if it break yo’ foot yo’ foot’ll git well, but if yo’ foot break *it*﻿—yes﻿—them’s chests and y’ needn’t think they ain’t valuable and that you can scrape ’em up bad as you please jes’ because they ain’t go not paint on ’em and got the hinges on th’ outside; they come from Siam or some them places Mr. Fred was where the folks is all colored but won’t admit it and you carry ’em by puttin’ two broomsticks through the sides, but deed I ain’t got no broomsticks f’ y’all to scratch up and break﻿—they have their own kings and queens and ev’rything jes’ like in the Bible, only I say colored folks ain’t got no business tryin’ to act white ’cause it always gets ’em into trouble﻿—where’s that other boy﻿—that big one come with y’all? Why don’t he turn in and help?﻿—he’s big enough﻿—ought to be ’shamed o’ hisse’f lettin’ y’all do all the work﻿—Ain’t we been had the worst summer rainin’ ev’ry day and look like it always had to ketch me outdoors with nothin’ on my head and you know what happens to this kind o’ hair when it gets wet﻿—”
“Whew-ee!” heaved Bubber. “Damn ’f that woman can’t talk d’ spots off d’ dice.”
“No lie. I ain’t got my breath back yet﻿—jes’ listenin’ to ’uh.”
“Yea﻿—and you tellin’ ’uh she could ride back with us if she wanted.”

===Chinese===
尽管富勒太太有那么一车嘱咐，货总归是装上了，每件家伙什一半裹着棉被，一半裹着那位太太的话语，等贝斯掉头回城走出老远，那些话还缭绕在货上不肯散：
"对——那件也装——那是他母亲的画像——就剩这一张了，千万仔细着点儿。丢了它，我知道他能死去。别的全丢了也得保住这张。仔细着——你们这些人从来不知道怎么拿东西，冲桌子腿打个喷嚏它们也得折——那是货真价实的红木桌子，你知道那多值钱——留神那个花瓶！——瞧你们拿东西这架势，谁都知道不是你们自个儿的。孩子，那花瓶比你一只脚还金贵——你的脚砸了它，脚还长得好；可你的脚要砸了*它*——对——那是几口箱子，你别当它们没上漆、合页安在外头就不值钱，就能由着性儿糟践；那是从暹罗来的，弗雷德先生去过的那些地方，那儿的人个个都是有色人，偏不肯认账；搬的时候要拿两根扫帚把从两边穿过去抬，可说真的，我没有扫帚把给你们糟蹋、弄断——那儿有他们自个儿的国王王后，样样齐全，跟圣经里一个样；不过依我说，有色人犯不上学白人充体面，学白人总没好下场——另一个小伙子呢——跟你们一块儿来的那个大块头呢？他怎么不上手帮忙？——他块头也够大了——让你们俩把活儿全包了，他也不害臊——这夏天可不是倒了霉，天天下雨，老天爷偏挑我头上什么也不戴站在外头的时候下，你知道这种头发淋了雨成什么德行——"
"嚯——！"布伯长喘一口气。"这娘们儿一开口，骰子上的点儿都能给她聊掉。"
"不骗你。光听她说，我这口气到现在还没倒上来。"
"是啊——你还跟她说，她要是愿意，可以搭咱们的车一块儿回城。"

===Original===
“Who?”
“You?”
“When?”
“Didn’ you stop and tell ’uh to come on, less go﻿—we was finished?”
“Oh yea. But I swear I thought I was talkin’ to you. Y’all look like sisters. If you and her didn’ have d’ same grandaddy, somebody played a awful dirty joke on y’ both.”
The inevitable quarrel ensued, and this somewhat took their minds off Bess’s unusual jogtrot. If the trip out had been slow, the trip back was endless. For out of all that had reached his ears, Shine remembered only one part of Arabella Fuller’s dyspneic discourse, and this hummed in his mind as persistent and unvarying as the rumble of Bess’s innards:

===Chinese===
"谁呀？"
"你呗？"
"几时？"
"你不是还停下来叫她一块儿走，说活儿干完了——？"
"哦，是了。可我起誓，我一直当是在跟你说话呢。你们俩长得跟姐妹似的。你跟她要不是同一个外公，那就是有人对你俩都使了个顶缺德的坏。"
照例的一场争吵接踵而至，这倒叫他们多少没留神贝斯这一反常态的小碎步。去程算慢的话，回程简直是没完没了。因为阿拉贝拉·富勒喘着气说的一车话，落进夏因耳朵里的只剩一句，这句在他脑子里嗡嗡打转，执拗又单调，跟贝斯五脏六腑的轰隆声一个样：

===Original===
“Go where, chile? Back to town with y’all? Deed I’ll have to stay out hyeh mos’ another week packin’ things f’ the winter. Y’all go right ahead, though﻿—Linda’s there and I done told ’er where to tell y’all to put ev’ything﻿—”
Linda living alone in the house with Fred Merrit, toper and dickty.
---
A piano is a malicious thing, the temporary dwelling of some evil spirit that follows you from one instrument to the next. Sooner or later that spirit catches you off guard and, using the instrument as its weapon, swiftly, viciously strikes. Either it gets you then and there or is itself permanently defeated.
Every man who enters this work thereby invites this pursuit. Both Jinx and Bubber had escaped for a time, but finally each had been caught. Bubber had lost a part of one foot. Jinx’s elbow had been crushed, leaving a permanent deformity. These injuries, however, did not materially hamper their work, and so Jinx and Bubber considered themselves fortunate; for, as the superstition had it, they now enjoyed immunity.

===Chinese===
"上哪儿去，孩子？跟你们回城？不成不成，我还得在这儿多待一个礼拜上下，把过冬的东西归置齐。你们只管先走——琳达在那儿呢，东西搁哪儿我都交代她了，让她指给你们——"
琳达独自住在那宅子里，守着弗雷德·梅里特——一个酒鬼，一个体面人。
---
钢琴是种歹毒的东西，是某个恶灵的临时居所，这恶灵跟着你，从一架琴游到下一架琴。早晚有一天它趁你不备，拿琴当凶器，又快又狠地下手。要么当场要了你，要么它自己从此一败涂地。
干上这一行的，等于自己招来这场追杀。金克斯和布伯都逃脱过一阵子，可到头来一个也没跑脱。布伯缺了小半只脚，金克斯的肘子被压碎过，落了个一辈子的畸形。不过这些伤并不怎么碍干活，于是两人自觉还算走运；照行里那个迷信的说法，他们从此有了免疫。

===Original===
Shine had so far gone without a scratch, had never been caught off guard. It was Jinx and Bubber’s belief that he would probably go on escaping. What chance did any piano have at a steel man lined with cast-iron? Shine was just as hard toward things as toward people, no more vulnerable in the one case than the other, and though ordinarily he could afford to be more generous and genial than most men, who dared not thus risk imposition, still in a pinch he was known to be more unyielding than bedrock. Nothing fazed Shine. “Remember how he held on to that piano the day the roof broke?”
But today for the first time Shine’s preoccupation put him quite off guard; and so today his evil pursuer struck.
---
The piano was an elderly upright which Merrit kept because it had been his first luxury. It was to go to the front room on the third floor of the house, a room which had been set apart as a remote and private playground﻿—a combination of den, poker room and too-bad-party resort. The instrument stood alone and sullen at the edge of the cluttered sidewalk, aloof, superior, apart, permitting the lesser pieces to go first.
Shine likewise aloof and apart, refused to enter the house with the others. He saw Linda only once, when first she gave Jinx admittance, and although he did not allow himself to make frank observations, he was aware from many a covert glance that the girl had withdrawn into the inner regions, evidently as intent upon avoiding him as was he upon avoiding her.
The time soon came, however, when all but the piano had been removed. Shine’s active participation had so far consisted only in handing things down from the van. Now he must direct the hoisting and so lend a more active hand.

===Chinese===
夏因至今连块油皮都没蹭破，从没让人钻过空子。金克斯和布伯都认定，他八成能一直逃下去。一架钢琴，撞上一个钢打铁衬的人，能有什么胜算？夏因对东西跟对人一样硬，两边不见得哪边更好下手；平日里他倒比多数人慷慨随和——旁人可不敢这么冒被人欺负的险——可事到紧要关头，谁都知道他比基岩还拧。什么都镇不住夏因。"还记得那天屋檐塌了，他抓着那架钢琴不撒手不？"
可是今天，夏因的心事头一回让他门户大开；于是今天，那个恶灵下手了。
---
这架琴是台上了年头儿的立式琴，梅里特留着它，因为这是他这辈子头一件奢侈品。它要进的是宅子三楼的前屋——那屋子专门辟作一处幽僻的私人乐园，书房、牌室兼"闹得太凶的聚会"场子，三合一。这大家伙阴沉沉独自杵在乱糟糟的人行道边上，冷着脸，端着架子，孤芳自赏，由着那些次一等的家什先行。
夏因也同样冷着脸、离着群，不肯跟大伙儿一道进宅子。他只见过琳达一回，就是她起初开门放金克斯进去那会儿；他虽不许自己明着张望，却借着一次次偷觑知道，姑娘已经退到宅子深处去了，显然一心要躲他，正如他一心要躲她。
可没多久，除了钢琴，东西就都搬完了。到这时为止，夏因出的力也就是从车上一件件往下递。如今吊装的活儿归他指挥，想不搭把手也不行了。

===Original===
It was now that his brooding inadvertence combined with his recently assumed recklessness to make him do an unprecedented thing. During his two years of working with Jinx and Bubber he had not once trusted either of them to anchor hoisting tackle. But now, instead of going to the roof of the house to anchor the tackle himself, he ordered Bubber to do so in his place. He’d be damned if Linda should think he was trying to see her.
“Well﻿—what the hell’s holdin’ y’?” he inquired as Bubber hesitated, doubting that he had heard aright. Bubber turned slowly, shaking his head and meditating aloud:
“When that boogy gits evil he gits so evil. They’s so damn much of ’im.”
“Then come down and unsash that winder,” Shine commanded balefully, “and stand by to pull in, see?”
Jinx would have followed to check up on his confrère’s technic, but Shine halted him to give further orders.
“You keep them flat feet o’ yours right on the sidewalk and hold on to this guide rope. I’ll do the pullin’. When Squatty pulls in up there you can go up and help him take it down.”

===Chinese===
就在这当口，他那副失魂落魄，掺上新近添的不管不顾，撺掇他干出一件破天荒的事。跟金克斯和布伯搭伙两年，固定吊装滑轮组的活儿，他一回也没信得过他们哪个。可这会儿，他非但没亲自上房顶去固定，反倒支使布伯替他去。他要是让琳达当他存心想瞧她，他誓不为人。
"喂——你他妈磨蹭什么呢？"布伯迟疑着没动，疑心自己听岔了，夏因便喝问。布伯慢吞吞回过身，摇着头，大声自言自语：
"那老黑一犯邪可真犯邪。他那身个头又那么他妈的一大。"
"那你下来，把那扇窗的窗框卸了，"夏因恶声恶气地下令，"守在边上往里拽，明白？"
金克斯本想跟上去查验同伙的手艺，夏因却叫住他，另有吩咐。
"你那两只扁平脚就钉在人行道上，攥住这根导索。拽的活儿我来。等矮墩墩在上头往里拽了，你再上去帮他卸。"

===Original===
So it was arranged, and presently Bubber, directed to the roof by the red-hottest mamma that had ever smiled upon him, was casting about for anchorage. A cylindrical airduct presented itself as the most likely object to use; it was well away from the front ledge of the roof; giving good purchase, it was of ample height and diameter, and it was apparently constructed of heavy cast-iron, cold, black, and shiny. As a matter of fact it was made of glazed mortar and had a hidden joint just below the roof.
To this duct Bubber made his major attachments, using many windings of line and an intricate system of knots; and for double security he carried the line ten feet further rearward to a chimney and around this wound the rest of it, fastening it uncompromisingly with a second complex of knots. When he tossed his tackle line over the edge, it was with the air of one who is sure that at least *his* end of the job has been well done.
Shine, on the sidewalk, had surrounded the uncovered piano with a girdle of quilting, and about this, somewhat loosely, had adjusted a single belt. Now he hooked the block into a ring fastened to the top of this belt. Then, with quite unnecessary vigor, he took hold and began yanking on the pulley. Jinx held the guide rope. The piano began to rise.
It rose in a succession of small, upward jerks, each epitomizing the vehement force that Shine imparted to the pulley line. That force, increased by the piano’s weight, extended to the anchorage on the roof, and the joint of the airduct in the floor of the roof felt and responded to each impulse from Joshua Jones’ inner conflict, heard and answered each wanton effort to vent through muscle what could not escape through mind. An even ascent that joint might have borne, a jerky one it could not; there was no question of whether it would snap, but simply of when.
Shine’s malevolent pursuer chose to decide this important question: the piano was just short of the end of its journey when the break came. Shine, getting a sort of satisfaction out of prodigious effort, gave an especially tremendous tug﻿—to find resistance vanish so suddenly that he pitched forward on his face still holding the line. He heard Jinx utter a terrified “Jesus!” and as he rolled over, instinctively attempting to clear the pathway of the falling instrument, he glimpsed it swaying above, knew that a second “safety” anchoring was all that gave him that instant’s doubtful grace, and heard a girl scream, “It’s slipping out﻿—! Quick﻿—it’s slipping out!”

===Chinese===
事情就这么定了。不多会儿，布伯便领了差遣上房顶，四处物色固定点——下差的那位，够得上他笑脸领教过的娘们儿里头火气最冲的一号。一根圆柱形的通气管看着最像样：离房前沿远远的，吃得住劲，个头粗细也够，瞧那材质分明是厚重的铸铁，又冷又黑又亮。实际上它却是釉浆砌的，紧贴房顶底下还藏着一道接缝。
布伯把主固定点安在这根管子上，绳子缠了一圈又一圈，打的结自成一套繁复学问；为求双保险，他又把绳牵出十英尺开外，绕到一座烟囱上，将余下的统统缠上去，再配上第二套繁复的绳结，钉是钉铆是铆地拴死。他把吊索往檐下一抛，那神气，分明是笃信这活儿至少*他*这一头干得漂亮。
人行道上，夏因给卸了包装的钢琴围了一圈棉被腰带，又在这圈上松松垮垮地扣了条皮带。他把滑轮钩进皮带顶上固定的铁环，接着攥住轮索，使出一股全用不着的蛮力，一下一下狠拽起来。金克斯攥着导索。钢琴开始往上升。
钢琴一蹿一蹿地往上挪，每一蹿都是夏因灌进轮索的那股狠劲的缩影。这股劲，再加上钢琴自身的分量，一路传到房顶的固定点上；那根通气管贴着房顶的暗缝便一下下地感受、一下下地应和着约书亚·琼斯内心冲突的每一次冲撞，听见并回应着他每一记任性的发力——心里出不去的，就由肌肉来撒。匀匀地上升，那道缝兴许还扛得住；这么一顿一顿地蹿，它扛不住；问题不在断不断，只在几时断。
夏因那个恶毒的追杀者，偏挑这时候来裁决这个要紧问题：钢琴眼看只差一寸就要到站，断裂来了。夏因正从那惊天动地的力气里咂摸出一种痛快，猛地又狠命一拽——只觉手上一空，阻力倏然消失，他攥着绳子扑了个嘴啃泥。他听见金克斯失声喊了句"老天爷！"，就势一滚，本能地想闪开那件下坠家什的来路，抬眼瞥见它在头顶上晃，明白全靠第二道"保险"固定，才换来这命悬一线的一瞬，接着就听见一个姑娘尖叫："滑脱了——！快——滑脱了！"

===Original===
The second anchorage held, but the initial drop had been enough to displace the soft girdle and belt from the center toward one end of the instrument. There was an instant’s hesitancy, as if to give direction, and abruptly the belt released the piano, which dropped like a live thing freed; plunged with a drive to crush and kill, like a beast pouncing on witless prey. The crash was like no other sound on earth﻿—explosion, groan, and whine﻿—thick wood, coarse metal, taut wires﻿—a noise that struck and shattered itself, then rose, spread, and hovered. It was as if a corner of hell had been blasted off and a thousand souls swarmed out, wailing.
Shine stood erect, looking dazedly about, touching an abrasion over one eye with exploratory fingers. And miraculous as a vision, Linda was before him, breathless with horror, apprehension, relief, with the effort of reaching him so quickly.
“Honey﻿—” she said, and found that nothing more would come.
“I’m all right﻿—Gee﻿—if you hadn’ ’a’ hollered﻿—”
“Oh﻿—” she managed, “I was at the window﻿—upstairs﻿—” and stood there a while in silence. Then because words failed, because something pinioned her arms that wanted to reach out to him, and because her eyes and throat mysteriously and ridiculously filled, she had a blank moment in which to realize how silly and impetuous she was, and another in which to be ashamed and take swift refuge in the house.
Shine on one side, Jinx on the other, looked down upon the wreckage. The piano lay half supine in a grotesque angular posture, its row of white keys gleaming like teeth, the lid of its keyboard sprung back and fixed, like the retracted upper lip of a creature that has died in agony.

===Chinese===
第二道固定撑住了，可先前那一下下坠，已把松软的棉被腰带连同皮带从琴身中间蹭到了一头。它迟疑了一瞬，像是在选定方向，随即皮带撒开了钢琴；钢琴像脱了栓的活物直坠而下，带着压碎人、砸死人的冲劲，如猛兽扑向蒙头蒙脑的猎物。那声巨响世上无物可比——爆炸、呻吟、锐啸——厚木板、粗铁件、绷紧的钢丝——一声砸下去又把自己摔碎的巨响，随后腾起、散开、悬在半空。像地狱被轰掉了一角，一千个鬼魂涌出来，哭嚎一片。
夏因直挺挺站着，晕晕乎乎四下张望，伸着手指摸探一只眼上方的擦伤。恍如显圣一般，琳达出现在他跟前，惊、怕、庆幸，加上一路飞奔赶到他身边的吃力，让她喘不上气来。
"亲人——"她说，便再也说不出一个字。
"我没事——哎哟——多亏你喊了那一嗓子——"
"哦——"她勉强接上，"我在窗口——楼上——"便那么默默站了一会儿。随后，因为话跟不上了，因为有什么攥住了她那双想朝他伸出去的胳膊，还因为她的眼睛和喉咙莫名其妙又可笑地满了上来，她先是空了一瞬，明白自己多么痴傻、多么莽撞，又空了一瞬，害起臊来，一转身飞快躲进宅子里去了。
夏因站在这边，金克斯站在那边，低头看那堆残骸。钢琴半仰着，蜷成一个怪诞的棱角姿势，一排白键森森亮着，像一口牙；琴盖向后弹开、僵在那里，像一头痛苦死去的畜生翻起的上唇。

===Original===
Jinx gave forth a prayer of thanksgiving:
“It sho’ as hell meant to get y’﻿—but it’s long gone now.”
Shine remained silent and contemplative.
Bubber came down. He and Jinx ejaculated comments. Bubber came over to palpate Shine and ask how the hell he ever missed it. A small crowd was gathering. People were looking out of windows.
“Crazy as hell,” Shine muttered absently. “ ‘Honey’﻿—Well, I’ll be john-browned﻿—” His hand again touched the raw place over his eye. “Little cold water wouldn’t do it no harm﻿—” And following in Linda’s wake, he too entered the house.

===Chinese===
金克斯发出一段感恩祷告：
"这玩意儿分明是铁了心要收拾你——好在如今送它见阎王了。"
夏因一言不发，若有所思。
布伯下来了。他跟金克斯你一言我一语地惊叹。布伯凑过来把夏因周身捏了捏，问他他妈的究竟是怎么躲过去的。一小群人围拢来。各家的窗户里都探出了脑袋。
"真是邪了门了，"夏因心不在焉地嘟哝。"‘亲人’——嘿，我这算是让约翰·布朗了——"他的手又摸上眼上那块破皮的地方。"拿点凉水敷敷总没坏处——"说着，他跟着琳达的脚印，也进了宅子。

### XX / 二十

===Original===
That Shine should visit a hospital when he felt almost perfectly well meant that some decided difference had come about in him. The scramble which had delivered him from grave injury had had no more serious visible effect than to abrade his hands and forehead against the cement, but it marked a conscious internal change which first came to light when he followed Linda into the house. Shine, the disciple of hardness, would not in any imaginable situation have been guilty of a surrender like that. Now again the change appeared when he decided that maybe he’d better go on ’round to the man’s clinic and let one them doctors look him over﻿—might even be some bones broke, who could tell?
He sat at one end of a white metal pew, an article of hospital furniture as uncomfortable in fact as it is in suggestion, and awaited his turn. Funny kid, Linda. Come runnin’ out there yesterday, scared clean white, then didn’ do a damn thing but turn around and go back. But “Honey﻿—?” Yea. He fell for that. And when he went in the house for water﻿—huh﻿—she was like as if nothing had happened. Showed him the sink and let him wash his head and gave him a towel﻿—but not another word. Honey. Yea. When he had stalled around as long as he could, he too said, “Well, honey﻿—” And all she answered﻿—didn’t need to be so tight about it, either﻿—was, “You better go see a doctor and make sure you’re all right.” Damned if he would. But here he was﻿—a whole day late, but here.
Since Harlem Hospital was in a state of transition, it happened that, of the two interns on the service, one was white with brown angora goat-hair and the other brown with black sheep’s wool. A blank white door opened, a patient was ejected and the white intern beckoned summarily to Shine.

===Chinese===
夏因自觉几乎毫发无伤，却肯跑一趟医院，这就说明他身上起了某种了不得的变化。那一场死里逃生的连滚带爬，看得见的后果不过是两手和额头在水泥地上蹭破了皮，可它标出了一处心知肚明的内变——头一回露头，是他跟着琳达走进宅子那会儿。夏因是硬字号的门徒，任凭怎么设想，也犯不下那样的缴械。如今这变化又露了一回头：他拿定主意，兴许还是绕到那位先生的诊所去，让随便哪位大夫给瞧瞧——没准儿还真断了根骨头，谁说得准呢？
他在一张白铁皮长椅的一头坐下等叫号——医院里这种家什，听着硌人，坐着更硌人。琳达这丫头，真有意思。昨天一路跑出来，吓得煞白，结果屁也没干一个，转身又回去了。可那声"亲人——？"呢。是啊，他吃这一套。等他进屋去要点水——嗬——她倒像没事人一样。指给他水槽，让他洗了头，递他一条毛巾——再多一句话没有。亲人。是啊。他磨蹭到实在磨蹭不下去，也说了句"那什么，亲人——"她回他的——也犯不着那么绷着——就一句："你最好去看看大夫，确认自己没事。"他偏不去。可人还是来了——足足晚了一整天，可到底是来了。
哈莱姆医院正处在过渡状态，于是这一科的两名实习医生，一位是白人，长一头棕色安哥拉山羊毛；另一位是棕色人，长一头黑绵羊毛。一扇白得没有一点表情的门开了，吐出一名病人，那位白人实习生朝夏因简短地一招手。

===Original===
Shine looked at him a moment then said: “I’ll wait for th’ other doctor.”
He settled back in his pew. Sweet kid, though, no lie. All women are funny, but you can overlook that﻿—if they’re good-looking enough. And Lindy was sure good to gaze on. Skin like honey﻿—honey with red cherries in it. Clear like thin wax with light behind it. You could almost see through it﻿—you could see through it﻿—you could see red flowers behind it; and when she got excited over anything it seemed that somebody waved the flowers back and forth. Like in the Casino that night, or that Sunday on the corner of Court Avenue. Gee﻿—! Eyes, too. Talk about eyes! Looking into her eyes was like looking into the sky at night﻿—looking from the bottom of an airshaft: deep, soft, and awfully black, with bright little stars twinkling away off﻿—That night on the Drive﻿—Judas Priest!
“Next!” called the brown intern cheerfully from a different blank white door, and Shine found himself in a clinical dressing room with tables and screens about and a little bed on wheels in one corner. There were mysterious varicolored bottles and jars and wickedly gleaming instruments everywhere, and the odor of phenol and iodoform took all the humor out of the air.
Not, however, for the intern nor the dressing-nurse who assisted him, a little round, stiffly starched brown doll-baby who should have been in a toyshop window.
“Yes, *sir*,” said the intern to the nurse, watching as she soaped and dried Shine’s forehead. “Best looking girl that’s been in this place since the man said, ‘Let’s have Harlem.’ Came in last night late. Ward VII. I sure mean to see her again.”

===Chinese===
夏因打量了他片刻，说："我等另一位大夫。"
他往长椅里一靠，接着等。不过那丫头是真甜，不骗人。女人个个古怪，可这也能睁一只眼闭一只眼——只要模样够俊。琳迪这张脸，确实怎么看怎么好。皮肤像蜜——蜜里泡着红樱桃。透亮得像点着灯的薄蜡。你几乎能看穿它——你就是能看穿它——能看见它后头一团红花；她一为什么事上了心，就像有人在后头把那团花来回摇。像游乐场那晚，像法院大道街角的那个礼拜天。嚯——！还有那双眼睛。眼睛才有的说！往她眼睛里看，就像夜里望天——像从通风井底往上望：又深，又软，黑得要命，远处亮着眨眼的小星星——滨河大道那一晚——老天爷！
"下一位！"那位棕色实习生从另一扇白门里喜洋洋地叫，夏因一抬脚，人已置身一间诊室换药房：四下是台子和屏风，墙角一张带轮子的小床。到处是五颜六色、来历不明的瓶瓶罐罐和闪着凶光的器械，石炭酸和碘仿的气味把空气里的一点诙谐卷了个干净。
只是这气味熏不着实习生，也熏不着给他打下手的换药护士——一个圆滚滚、浆得笔挺的棕色小玩偶，本该摆在玩具店橱窗里。
"*可不是*，"实习生对护士说，眼睛瞧着她给夏因的额头上肥皂、擦干。"自打那位说‘要有哈莱姆’的那天起，这地方就没进来过这么俊的姑娘。昨晚深更半夜进来的。第七病房。我可是打定主意要再见她一面的。"

===Original===
“Ward VII﻿—oh Doctor﻿—not Ward VII!”
“What difference does it make? She can be cured.”
“Find out her name?”
“I didn’t miss. Young. Linda Young. And as soon as dressing clinic’s over, the doctor﻿—ahem!﻿—is going to take Miss Linda’s history. This fellow’s my last case. Oh boy﻿—how I love to take histories: Did you ever have measles, chickenpox, whooping cough, mumps, scarlet fever? How many children? Oh no﻿—of course not﻿—I meant brothers and sisters? How many nights a week do you have to yourself, and how? When were you last out with the boyfriend? And now you have a pain in the bottom of your stomach﻿—?”
The intern dabbed iodin on the denuded area of Shine’s forehead. An abrasion, baring the most sensitive nerve-ends, is nothing to dab iodin on without due consideration. But Shine might have been anesthetized for all the pain he felt. Linda in this hospital? Linda?﻿—What the﻿—? Linda?

===Chinese===
"第七病房——哎呀大夫——别是第七病房！"
"这有什么打紧？治得好就是了。"
"打听到她名字了吗？"
"一问一个准。姓扬。琳达·扬。等换药门诊一完，大夫——咳咳！——就要去问琳达小姐的病史了。这位老兄是我最后一号。嘿——问病史这事我可太爱了：出过麻疹没有？水痘？百日咳？腮腺炎？猩红热？生过几个孩子？哦不——当然没有——我是说兄弟姐妹有几个？一礼拜里几晚归你自己，怎么个归法？上回跟男朋友出门是几时？那么现在，你小肚子底下疼是吧——？"
实习生往夏因额头破皮的地方点碘酒。蹭掉一层皮、露出最娇嫩的神经末梢的地方，点碘酒本不该这么不假思索。可夏因疼是不疼的，跟上了麻药似的。琳达在这家医院里？琳达？——什么——？琳达？

===Original===
The intern finished his dressing after a fashion and bustled the dazed Shine out, hurrying on past him. The intern was on the way to more pleasant duties.
Shine, numbly incredulous, followed slowly in the same direction. The white uniform was soon lost in a tangle of other white uniforms. Shine wandered on. Ward VII. Oh yes, you want the G.Y.N. service? Down that way, turn left then right﻿—Looking for some place, mister? Ward VII? Second floor north. Ward VII? Right around the corner﻿—yes, you’ll see the sign﻿—if you look﻿—
Ward VII. Yes, this is Ward VII. Whom do you wish to see? Linda Young﻿—yes, she’s a new patient﻿—Have you a card? She’s a ward case you know, and visiting hours are over. I’m sorry, but you’ll have to go back and get a card.
He went back to get a card. Miss Linda Young on Ward VII. Was it a relative? No. Just a friend? Sorry. Couldn’t be issuing cards all day. Come at visiting hour tomorrow﻿—two to three p.m. Very sorry, but it was really against the rules. Find out how she is for you, if you like. Click﻿ ﻿… G.Y.N.?﻿ ﻿… How is Linda Young?﻿ ﻿… Yes. Resting comfortably?﻿ ﻿… Click. Resting comfortably.
Scarcely able to sense direction, Shine wandered away through a labyrinth of hallways. Linda resting comfortably﻿—what kind of a joke, for Pete’s sake﻿—He was completely lost when, after a long time, he met a familiar figure, the intern who had dressed his wound and gone off to consult Linda Young. He caught the intern by the arm﻿—had a crazy impulse to laugh at the way in which the intern shrank from that apparent attack. The intern had quite forgotten him.

===Chinese===
实习生马马虎虎上完药，把晕头转向的夏因打发了出去，自己紧赶着从他身边超了过去。实习生这是奔更愉快的公务去了。
夏因木然不信，顺着同一方向慢慢跟。那身白制服很快缠进一树别的白制服里，认不出来了。夏因接着瞎转。第七病房。哦，您是找妇科（G.Y.N.）吧？往那边，先左再右——找哪儿，先生？第七病房？二楼北边。第七病房？拐角就是——对，看见牌子就对了——只要您肯看——
第七病房。对，这里是第七病房。您找哪位？琳达·扬——对，新来的病人——有探视牌吗？她算大病房的贫额病人您知道，探视时间也过了。对不起，您得回去领张牌。
他回去领牌。第七病房的琳达·扬小姐。是她亲属吗？不是。只是朋友？抱歉。牌子不能一整天一整天地发。明天探视时间再来吧——下午两点到三点。实在抱歉，这实在是坏规矩。愿意的话，替您问问她的情况。咔嗒。……妇科？……琳达·扬情况如何？……是。躺着还安稳？……咔嗒。躺着还安稳。
夏因东南西北都辨不出，在迷宫一样的走廊里飘。琳达"躺着还安稳"——老天爷，这开的什么玩笑——转了半晌，他彻底迷了路，却撞见一个熟脸——正是给他上药、又奔琳达·扬而去的那个实习生。他一把攥住实习生的胳膊——瞧对方像遭了明抢似的往回缩，他竟起了一股发疯的笑的冲动。实习生早把他忘了个干净。

===Original===
“Listen, doc﻿—Linda Young﻿—Ward VII﻿—I want to know about her.”
“You want to know about her? Know what?”
“Is it really her?”
“Really her? What the﻿—Do you know her?”
“She﻿—yea﻿—I know her well.”

===Chinese===
"听着，大夫——琳达·扬——第七病房——我想知道她的事。"
"你想知道她的事？知道什么？"
"真是她吗？"
"真是她？搞什么——你认识她？"
"她——是啊——熟得很。"

===Original===
“Oh﻿—so you’re the guy?” There was untold scorn in the intern’s voice.
“Me? What guy? Is she hurt?”
The intern looked him over cynically. “You ought to know.”
“Know? Know what, doc? I didn’ even know she was sick. I saw her yesterday. She was all right yesterday.”
“What time yesterday did you see her?”

===Chinese===
"哦——原来就是你这家伙？"实习生的话音里有说不尽的鄙夷。
"我？什么家伙？她伤着了？"
实习生冷冷地把他从头到脚打量一遍。"你该如何道。"
"知道？知道什么，大夫？她病了我都不知道。我昨天还见着她了。昨天她好好儿的。"
"昨天几点见的她？"

===Original===
“Early afternoon.”
“Early afternoon. Oh. Well﻿—she came in late last night. You didn’t see her last night?”
“No. Why? What’s wrong with her?”
“Nothing much. Only some guy ought to get his block knocked off.”
“What you talkin’ ’bout, doc?”

===Chinese===
"晌午头里。"
"晌午头里。哦。那么——她是昨天深更半夜进来的。昨晚你没见着她？"
"没有。怎么了？她出什么事了？"
"没什么大事。只不过有个家伙，脑袋该叫人拧下来。"
"大夫，你说的是什么话？"

===Original===
“Tell me﻿—is this your girl?”
“She ain’t nobody else’s?”
“Well then, you ought to know this. Some guy found her alone last night where she works, see, and tried to﻿—show her a deep point. He couldn’t make her listen to reason, so he tried caveman stuff. There was quite a scuffle. The girl got loose and out of the house, but she keeled over on the sidewalk before she got two blocks away. Scared dumb. She was brought in with a diagnosis of assault with intent﻿—”
The change that distorted Shine’s face told the intern he had gone far enough. The features writhed, the bronze skin seemed to have suddenly been dusted with ashes, and there was unquestionable intent to kill in his eyes and the whole attitude of his body. The intern, too late as he now realized, tried to mitigate his story:

===Chinese===
"你告诉我——这是你姑娘？"
"她不是别人的，还能是谁的？"
"那这事你就该如何道了。昨天夜里有个家伙，撞见她一个人在东家干活，明白不，就想——给她开示一条深入的道理。讲不通，就换了穴居人的手段。好一场厮打。姑娘挣脱出来，逃出了宅子，可没跑出两个街区，就一头栽倒在人行道上。吓哑了。送进来的时候，诊断写的是蓄意强暴——"
夏因脸上那阵扭曲的变化告诉实习生：话说到这儿就够了。五官扭动，青铜色的脸皮像忽然蒙了一层灰，那双眼睛里、那副身架里，明明白白是杀气。实习生这才明白过来，只是太迟了，赶紧把话往轻里描：

===Original===
“It’s all right of course﻿—he didn’t succeed. She’s just got a sprained ankle and a little shock﻿—”
But Shine brushed past and moved away in huge, infuriate strides. Even far down the corridor he looked the size of ten men.
The intern watched him swing out of sight, then shrugged his shoulders helplessly. “I wonder,” he asked himself, “when I’ll learn some sense?”

===Chinese===
"当然没出事——他没能得手。她不过是脚脖子崴了，人受了点惊——"
可夏因一擦身冲了出去，迈着盛怒的大步走远。哪怕在走廊尽头望过去，他也像有十个人那么大。
实习生望着他一晃一晃走出视野，无可奈何地耸了耸肩。"真不知道，"他问自己，"我哪天才学得乖一点？"

## Battle / 战斗

### XXI / 二十一

===Original===
Overnight Fred Merritt’s Court Avenue house had become a ghastly ruin. Every pane had been bashed in with flood, every window frame charred with fire, each of the gray stone window margins frayed and blackened with smoke. Yesterday these windows had surveyed the world serenely, bright and alive. Today they looked like the deep, dark-circled orbits of sunken blind eyes.
The place had been gutted, heart and bowels. Its vitals, whatever things had given it substance, circulation, and life, all had been hopelessly battered and crushed till they’d shrunk out of sight: One could stand on the sidewalk and see the sunset through and beyond the rear wall﻿—a hard broad grin of a sunset, which transilluminated the flame sacked dwelling, mocking its emptiness without pity, deriding its devastation. When eventually the sun’s grin faded out, it was as if a contemptuous amused observer had at last turned aside and gone off on more important business.
The house stood stark as a corpse in the shrouding dusk.
---

===Chinese===
一夜之间，弗雷德·梅里特在法院大道上的宅子变成了一片惨不忍睹的废墟。每一块窗玻璃都叫大水砸得粉碎，每一副窗框都叫火燎得焦黑，每一道灰色石头的窗沿也熏得绽了边、上了乌。昨天，这些窗子还亮堂堂、活泛泛地静观世界；今天，它们已经像一双双塌陷的盲眼，眼窝又深又黑。
这宅子被开了膛、破了肚，掏了心肝五脏。它的命脉——那些曾给它筋骨、血行和活气的一切——全被打得稀烂、压得粉碎，缩得没了影儿：人站在人行道上，穿过断墙就能望见那头的落日——一轮咧着大嘴的落日，把这座被火洗劫的宅子照了个通透，无情地嘲弄它的空，讥笑它的败落。到后来太阳那副狞笑淡了下去，就像一位又鄙夷又看着好笑的看客终于掉头走开，忙更要紧的公事去了。
宅子在四合的暮色里直挺挺立着，像一具裹了尸布的僵尸。
---

===Original===
It was upon this scene that Shine came, less frantic now, but no less grim than when he had left the hospital earlier in the day. Even then he had realized that Merrit would not be found at home during the day, and had finished his afternoon’s work in a silent turmoil. Added delay had not subdued his fury﻿—had merely stored up a greater potential violence, like added tension on a spring. Now, when he unexpectedly came upon this ruin, it was as if the spring suddenly cracked.
He stood on the sidewalk looking up at the looming gray carcass of a house. For a moment it took his breath. Twilight made it the more indistinct﻿—he craned his neck forward and stared; looked all about him to verify the neighborhood, walked forward to a point where he could discern the number on the house next door﻿—315﻿—came back, stood in a stupor of unbelief; and after a while heaved a great sigh of reluctant, bitter conviction:
“Damn if the fays didn’t get ’im,” he muttered. “The dirty﻿—” For the time being his present mission of vengeance was submerged in the onrush of a greater hatred, a hatred more deeply ingrained and of far longer standing; for the moment he glared insanely around at the cool, still, empty street and at the rows of serene gray houses standing side by side. They gave forth a maddening impression of distance and unconcern. They looked quite satisfied. This catastrophe was for them the answer to all their prayers. Now that it was done, they could go on as they always had. The ruined dwelling had simply earned and received the wages of sin﻿—If Shine could have trampled and crushed them all in that moment, he would surely have done so.
But as this tide of hatred fell and receded, his original murderous intent emerged like a spire through abating flood. What if they had got Merrit? A guy like Merrit deserved everything he got. And he hadn’t got half what was coming to him yet﻿—not if he could be found﻿—Linda resting comfortably﻿—the dickty liquor-head﻿—
He knew it. Merrit had meant to put it on her ever since that first morning here on Court Avenue﻿—the morning she strode past like a million dollars, ignoring Jinx and Bubber’s comments. “Figgerin’ on a jive already﻿—the doggone dickty hound. Why the hell can’t dickties stick to their own women, ’stead o’ messin’ ’round some honest workin’ girl?” That was the thought he had got from the way Merrit looked at her that morning﻿—Well﻿—it wouldn’t be long now﻿—let him get one hand on that yellow throat﻿—just let him sink the fingers of one hand into it﻿—just let him take the bastard’s ankle in his hands and twist it off﻿—

===Chinese===
夏因来到的就是这么一片景象跟前。他此刻不那么狂躁了，可比起白天离开医院那阵，阴沉分毫不减。那时他就明白，白天在梅里特家里是堵不着人的，于是闷头把下午的活儿干完，心里一路翻江倒海。这番耽搁并没有消他的火——只是把更大的暴力攒了起来，像弹簧又上了一圈劲。此刻冷不丁撞见这片废墟，那弹簧咔嚓一声断了。
他站在人行道上，仰头望着那具灰扑扑、高耸耸的房子残躯。一时连气都喘不上来。暮色把它抹得愈发模糊——他伸着脖子瞪眼看；又四下张望，核对这个街坊；再走到能辨清隔壁门牌的地方——315——又退回来，站在一种不敢相信的懵懂里；过了半晌，才长长叹出一口不情愿的、发苦的认命：
"真他妈是白佬们把他收拾了，"他咕哝，"这帮脏——"一时间，他这趟寻仇的差事被一股更大的恨意冲到了水底——那股恨扎得更深、年头也长得多的恨；此刻他疯了似的环视这条清冷空寂的街，和那一排肩并肩立着的、安详的灰房子。它们散发出一股叫人发疯的疏远和漠然。它们瞧着相当满意。这场浩劫，在它们正是所有祷告的应验。如今事已办成，它们大可以照旧过下去。那栋被毁的宅子，不过是挣下了罪的工钱、如数领走了罢了——这一刻，夏因若能把它们统统踩进地里碾个稀烂，他一定干得出来。
可这股恨潮一落一退，他原本那桩杀人的念想便像退洪里露出的塔尖。白佬们收拾了梅里特又怎样？梅里特那种家伙，落什么都是活该。他该得的还欠着一多半呢——只要还找得着人——琳达"躺着还安稳"——那个体面的酒鬼——
他早就知道。打法院大道的头一个早晨起，梅里特就打上了她的主意——那个早晨，她像揣着一百万现大洋似的从人前走过，金克斯和布伯的议论全当耳旁风。"早就惦记上手了——这条狗日的体面猎狗。体面人他妈为什么不守着自家的女人，偏要糟蹋人家规规矩矩做工的姑娘？"那天早上梅里特瞧她的那个眼神，就叫他咂摸出了这个意思——好——这日子长不了了——让他一只手先掐上那截黄脖子——只消让他五根指头扣进去——只消让他攥住那杂种的脚脖子一拧——

===Original===
But this house﻿—Hell a’mighty﻿—what a wreck﻿—
His turbulent emotions strangely dominated by curiosity, he slowly, almost fearfully made his way up the front stoop of the house. Shattered glass, strewn over the steps, crunched dryly under his feet. The doorways bounding the vestibule were open; the outer door a mere frame to which angular fragments of glass still clung like monstrous teeth; the inner a fallen barrier, shattered and blackened, prone on the floor.
He explored the front room, stepping cautiously over obstructing wreckage, just able to perceive in the dimness the utter, unsparing destruction; ceilings black, walls gray and water-soaked, woodwork a burnt-cork caricature, patches of plaster fallen away baring the carbonized understructure.
“Whoever done this sho’ knew his business﻿—the﻿—”
The floor was a clutter of water-soaked pieces, some still wrapped in burlap. A cabinet lay on its side in a corner, its upper half bared and blackened, its lower still embraced in a scorched, wet covering. Little puddles glistened here and there; a rug protested under Shine’s step with the squish of a full sponge, compressed. A besooted prism-chandelier still hung from the channeled ceiling, against the gray of which it was silhouetted like a shadow of itself. To the rim of the broad doorway leading from this room, there still hung traces of what had yesterday been portieres of metal brocade, now shreds of gray lace woven of cobwebs, the greater part fallen about the threshold, a scum of soft wet ash.

===Chinese===
可这宅子——我的老天——糟蹋成这样——
翻腾的情绪竟叫好奇压了势头，他慢慢腾腾、几乎是提心吊胆地摸上宅子正门的台阶。碎玻璃铺满台阶，在他脚底下咔咔地干响。围出前厅的两道门都敞着：外门只剩一副框子，角角棱棱的玻璃碴子还挂在上面，像一嘴怪物的牙；里门是一道倒下的屏障，砸烂了，烧黑了，直挺挺趴在地上。
他探看前厅，小心地跨过挡路的残骸，昏暗里勉强辨认出那份彻头彻尾、毫不留情的毁灭：天花板乌黑，墙壁灰败、浸透了水，木活成了一幅焦炭涂出来的漫画，成片的灰泥坠了地，露出烧成炭的里骨。
"干这活儿的家伙是真在行——这——"
地板上泡水的家什堆得下不去脚，有几件还裹着麻布。一口柜子侧翻在墙角，上半截焦黑赤裸，下半截还叫一块烧糊的湿布抱着。到处闪着小小的水洼；一张地毯在夏因脚下像块攥饱了水的海绵，咕唧一声表示抗议。一盏熏黑的棱晶吊灯还挂在开了槽的天花板上，衬着灰顶，映出它自己的一道影子。从这间屋通出去的宽门口上，还挂着昨日金属锦缎门帘的残迹，如今只剩蛛网织成的灰纱条，大半已经堆在门槛上，成了一摊软塌塌的湿灰。

===Original===
“This ain’t a damn thing compared to what I’ll do to him﻿—”
Shine moved through the foyer past a crumbled charcoal staircase, and on thence into the back room. This room, equally demolished, was narrower than the front, and presented at one side a doorless doorway leading into a small side room. Disregarding the settling darkness, Shine went over to this doorway, then suddenly halted, stood quite motionless, intent on an unexpected sight within that room.
The rear wall was almost entirely occupied by a tall broad window. There was a table before this window, and seated at the table, a man. Looking obliquely through the doorway, Shine saw that the man did not sit wholly erect, but slumped down in his chair as limply as if his backbone had melted, drooped there almost double, his head bowed forward on his chest. Despite this lifeless posture, it was possible to recognize the figure by the gray dusk of the window against which it was outlined. Shine knew that he was looking upon Fred Merrit.
He stared scowling a moment, bent forward a bit to catch some sign of life, and was on the point of approaching the figure when it moved in a curious way: shook like a man with a chill﻿—slumped quiet﻿—violently shook again. Slowly it dawned on Shine that maybe the bird was crying. And as he continued to stare and wonder on this unfamiliar sight, he became aware of something grasped in one of Merrit’s extended hands: a fairly large picture-frame, out of which the canvas had been burnt, leaving only a frayed, singed, marginal rim. Shine belabored his brain to catch an elusive memory of that frame, till it broke upon him that this was the one that had contained the likeness of Merrit’s mother; the one about which Mrs. Fuller had warned, “He’d die if he ever lost it.”

===Chinese===
"这跟我将要给他收拾的比，连个屁都不算——"
夏因穿过门斗，绕过一段酥成炭渣的楼梯，再往里进了后屋。这间屋子毁得同样干净，只是比前厅窄，一侧还留着一道没有门的门洞，通一间小侧屋。夏因顾不上暮色四合，径直走到那门洞前，却又猛地站住，一动不动，让屋里一副料想不到的景象钉在了那儿。
后墙几乎叫一扇又高又宽的窗子占满。窗前有张桌子，桌前坐着个人。斜穿过门洞望进去，夏因看见那人坐得并不端正，而是瘫在椅子里，软塌塌的，像脊梁骨化了，几乎对折地耷拉着，脑袋垂在胸口。尽管这姿势全无生气，衬着窗外灰蒙蒙的暮色勾出的那道轮廓，还是认得出他是谁。夏因知道，自己望着的正是弗雷德·梅里特。
他皱眉瞪了一阵，微微欠身想逮一点活气，正要朝那身影走过去，那人却古怪地动了起来：像打摆子似的抖一阵——瘫下去静一静——又猛地抖起来。夏因这才慢慢醒过闷儿来：兴许这家伙在哭。他接着盯接着琢磨这副生疏的光景，又看见梅里特一只摊开的手里攥着件东西：一个挺大的相框，框里的画布已烧得精光，只剩一圈磨毛燎焦的边。夏因翻搅脑浆，去够一段抓不住的记忆，直到猛地想起——这正是装梅里特母亲画像的那一只；正是富勒太太千叮万嘱"他要真丢了它，能死去"的那一只。

===Original===
For what seemed a long time Shine stood looking, things romping through his brain. Linda struggling﻿—no﻿—resting comfortably. The Goddamned dickty﻿—what happened? Fays got him﻿—dirty sneaks﻿—I *mean* they got him﻿—look at this place. Merrit. There he is﻿—what the hell﻿—crying﻿—Jesus﻿—that picture of his mother﻿—
Then Shine did what would have seemed to his associates an amazing, an unpardonable thing. There with the man he’d set out to punish alone, within his grasp, he stood silent, apparently undecided, made not a single move to strike. And after a while, slowly turned about and found his way out of the house.
It amazed Shine himself﻿—amazed him and chagrined him. He felt rather glad of the darkness outside﻿—it was a sort of balm for his shame. Hard boogy he was﻿—yea﻿—awful hard﻿—the hardest boogy in Harlem. There he was, this dickty, this guy that﻿—right there, crying to be crowned. And what does the hard guy do﻿—the hardest boogy in Harlem? He gets a seasick feeling in the belly and turns around and sneaks out!
He mumbled excuses to himself as he wandered away down the street:
“Hell﻿—I’ll get ’im later﻿—Gee﻿—y’ can’t hit a guy when he’s down﻿—”

===Chinese===
夏因站着瞧了半晌，脑子里万马奔腾。琳达挣扎——不——躺着还安稳。那个天杀的体面人——到底出了什么事？白佬收拾了他——一帮阴损的货——这回*可是真*收拾了——瞧瞧这宅子。梅里特。人在那儿呢——搞什么——哭呢——老天爷——他妈那画像——
接着夏因干了一件在他那帮伙计看来准得算离奇、准得算不可饶恕的事。那个他一路寻来要惩办的人就在眼前，孤身一个，任他发落，他却站着不响，一副拿不定主意的样子，一指头也没动。过了半晌，他慢慢转过身，摸出宅子去了。
连他自己都愣了——又惊又臊。外头的黑倒叫他松了口气——那黑像给他的臊抹了一层膏药。硬骨头老黑，他可是——是啊——硬得很——全哈莱姆最硬的老黑。人家就在那儿，那位体面人，那位——就在眼面前，哭得等人来摘脑袋。可这位硬汉子干了什么——全哈莱姆最硬的老黑？他肚子里泛起一阵晕船的劲儿，转过身，溜了！
他顺着街一路走远，嘴里给自己念叨托词：
"得了——赶明儿再收拾他——嗐——总不能趁人倒了再下家伙——"

### XXII / 二十二

===Original===
He was the first visitor to arrive on Ward VII the next afternoon. The odor of phenol and iodoform that had pervaded the clinic hovered here also. The beds were repellently white and orderly. There were only a few scattered patients﻿—ugly women in bath robes and mules.

He found Linda seated beside a bed; a profusion of cotton and gauze was piled at one end of the bed, and from these Linda, with great concentration and delicate, mysterious precision was fashioning oblong pads which she stacked at the opposite end. Her back was toward him, and he stood for a moment behind her, looking; and if yesterday he had had strange emotions watching the unaware Merrit, today his feelings were past understanding watching the unaware Linda. Nothing seemed to be wrong with her, yet the sight of her sitting there in that clean, sparse, terrible place, bending so intently over her task, made his breath stop in his throat, so that he had to swallow it deliberately before he could speak.

“Hello, Lindy.”

She turned and looked up, half rose, sank back; the stars came out in her eyes, which consumed him in unbelieving astonishment, and she gave a little catching laugh. “Why﻿—how’d you know I was here?”

He appropriated the chair beside the next bed and sat down.

===Chinese===
第二天下午，七号病房他是最早到的探视人。门诊部里弥漫的石炭酸和碘仿气味，这里也悬浮不散。病床白得拒人，铺陈整齐。稀稀落落只有几个病人——一群披浴袍、趿拖鞋的丑女人。

他找到琳达时，她正坐在一张床边；床的一头堆着大团大团的棉纱和绷带布，琳达全神贯注，用一种精细而神秘的准确手法，把它们做成一个个长方形的敷料垫，在床的另一头码成小垛。她背对着他，他在她身后站了一会儿，静静看着；昨天望着浑然不觉的梅里特，他心里已经翻腾起说不清的情绪；今天望着浑然不觉的琳达，那滋味更是没法究诘。她分明好好的，什么也没坏，可看着她坐在那间洁净、空落、骇人的屋子里，那样专心地俯身干着手头的活儿，他的呼吸便哽在喉咙里，不得不刻意咽上一口，才说得出话。

“嗨，琳蒂。”

她回头仰望，半撑起身，又落回去；她眼里倏地亮起星星，把他整个儿吞进难以置信的惊愕里，随即发出一声噎住似的轻笑。“怎么——你怎么知道我在这儿？”

他顺手占了邻床边一把椅子坐下。

===Original===
“Didn’t you tell me to go to the hospital?”

She quickly sought the iodin stained place on his forehead, reached impulsively toward it, checked the motion. “No﻿—’tisn’t s’posed to be touched, is it?”

“You﻿—all right, Lindy?”

“Great. Going home today. Wasn’t any sense in them bringing me here anyhow. I wasn’t﻿—I was only scared﻿—I guess.”

“That all?”

===Chinese===
“不是你叫俺上医院来的吗？”

她一眼找到他额头上那块碘酒渍，冲动地伸手要碰，又生生收住。“不能碰——那地方不许碰的，对吧？”

“你——没大事吧，琳蒂？”

“好着呢。今儿就回家。他们送我来本来就没道理。我没——我就是吓着了——大概是。”

“就这些？”

===Original===
“Well﻿—I hurt my ankle﻿—see?” She displayed a bandaged joint. “Not bad. Strapped. They’d transfer me to another ward if I wasn’t leaving so soon. How’d you know?”

“Doc in the clinic told me. Told me all about it.” There was silence. To relieve her embarrassment, evident by her averted face, he assured her, “He won’t pull nothin’ like that any more, Lindy.”

She was alarmed.

“You didn’t﻿—didn’t﻿—?”

“Nope. Not yet.”

===Chinese===
“还有——脚脖子崴了——瞧？”她给他看缠了绷带的脚踝。“不碍事。上了绷带。要不是马上出院，他们还得把我转到别的病房去。你怎么知道的？”

“诊所的大夫告诉俺的。原原本本都告诉俺了。”一阵沉默。她侧着脸，尴尬分明写在上面；为给她解围，他宽慰道：“他再不敢来那手了，琳蒂。”

她慌了。

“你没——没——？”

“没。还没。”

“Don’t!”

===Chinese===
“听着，宝贝儿。麻烦算老几，真到那一步，俺招的祸哪儿止这个数——”
